<table>

<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which plasma protein is the documented binding partner of this retinol-binding protein, with the association described as helping prevent its loss through kidney filtration?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Serum albumin<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Transthyretin (TTR)<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Transferrin<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Haptoglobin<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence provided corresponds to Retinol-Binding Protein 4 (RBP4), identified by its signal peptide (MEWVWALVLLAALGGGS) at the N-terminus and its characteristic ~200 amino acid length typical of secreted retinol-binding proteins.<br><br>RBP4 is a plasma protein (~21 kDa) that transports retinol (vitamin A) in the blood. Due to its small molecular weight, free RBP4 would normally be filtered through the glomerulus and lost in urine. To prevent this renal loss, RBP4 forms a non-covalent complex with transthyretin (TTR, also known as prealbumin), which is a ~55 kDa tetramer. The resulting RBP4-TTR complex has a molecular weight of approximately 150 kDa, which exceeds the size threshold for glomerular filtration (~60-70 kDa), thereby preventing the protein from being filtered and lost through the kidneys.<br><br>Evaluating the options:<br>- A. Serum albumin: While albumin is the most abundant plasma protein, it is not the documented binding partner of RBP4 for preventing renal filtration.<br>- B. Transthyretin (TTR): This is the well-established binding partner of RBP4. The RBP4-TTR interaction is a classic example of how protein-protein interactions can modulate renal clearance.<br>- C. Transferrin: This is an iron-transport protein with no documented role in RBP4 metabolism.<br>- D. C. Transferrin - This is an iron-transport protein, not related to RBP4

The answer is clearly B. Transthyretin (TTR).

The key biological fact here is that RBP4 forms a complex with Transthyretin (TTR, also known as prealbumin). This TTR-RBP complex is a heterotetramer (TTR₂RBP₂) with a molecular weight of approximately 67 kDa, which is above the glomerular filtration threshold. This complex formation is specifically documented to reduce renal loss of RBP4.

Let me evaluate each option:

A. Serum albumin - While albumin is the most abundant plasma protein, it is NOT the specific documented interaction partner for RBP4 that prevents glomerular filtration. Albumin&#x27;s role in plasma is as a general carrier protein, but the specific documented interaction with RBP4 is with TTR.

B. Transthyretin (TTR) - This is the correct answer. TTR specifically binds RBP4 to form a heterotetramer complex (MW ~67 kDa) that prevents glomerular filtration. This is well-documented in biochemistry literature. The TTR-RBP interaction is specific and physiologically significant.

C. Transferrin - This is an iron-binding protein and has no documented specific interaction with RBP4 for the purpose of preventing renal filtration.

D. Haptoglobin - This is a hemoglobin-binding protein involved in heme scavenging and has no documented specific interaction with RBP4.

The question specifically asks to &quot;Distinguish specific interaction evidence from a guess based only on shared presence in plasma.&quot; This is a clear hint that while albumin is present in plasma alongside RBP4, the SPECIFIC documented interaction partner is TTR.

The answer is B.
